Scenario:

A bank's loan approval system processes loan applications based on the
applicant's credit score. The system categorizes credit scores into three
groups:
 ^N99aiehW

•Poor Credit (0 - 500): Loan rejected
•Average Credit (501 - 700): Loan approved with standard interest rate
•Excellent Credit (701 - 850): Loan approved with a lower interest rate
Any score outside the range of 0-850 is considered invalid input.

Testing for Polgen Website ^7GTqOz04

1- Jest +Vitest
2-Apache
3-Seleinum
4-Manual Black box test (for whole features)
5- unit test (for all functionality)
6- BrowserStack (For testing on other browsers) ^Z3MZZyV5

Objectives:

    Validate core features (registration, order flows)

    Ensure usability and UI/UX consistency

    Detect bugs and performance issues

    Confirm security and data integrity

Testing Types:

    Primarily manual

    Selective automated testing (Selenium, Jest, Vitest)

    Performance testing (Apache JMeter)

    Security testing ( SQLMap) ye ye ye ye ^PbbeuDsP

We applied black-box  to test common user actions, edge cases, and  security or performance weaknesses.

    Test case design:

        Based on functional requirements: login, registration, form validation, admin approval

        Prioritized by impact : ( High: authentication ;  Medium: layout  ;  Low: IE11 ) 

    Special cases included:

       - SQL injection attempts

       - Session timeout behavior

       - Input validation and tooltip testing

    Note on IE11:

    Internet Explorer 11 (IE11) is an outdated browser that failed modern JS and layout tests.
    We included basic checks but marked its support as low priority since it's deprecated. ^0WKlrKLS

Operating systems: Windows 11, Arch Linux

Browsers tested: Chrome, Firefox, Edge, Safari, Opera, IE11

Device simulation: BrowserStack, Chrome DevTools

Backend environment: Node.js + Express

Frontend testing: React with (Jest/Vitest test tools)

Database: PostgreSQL   via CLI ^2EpLwfqx

✅ We managed bug tracking manually using Notion.

    Tracking format:

        Fields:  tester, date , description, priority, status, what will be done , image after done

    Examples:

        Mohamed Attia : 20/2 ->25/2 ,fixing the empty submission issue , high , 
       workingOn, implement a code to trackes each input,

Kerim Hariri, ELnur ALiyev, Mohamed ATTIA EID ATTIA EID, Enes Eryigit ^IpkMf9I5

Kerim Hariri :  was responsible for usability and performance testing. 
He verified user experience factors like alignment, 
  responsiveness, error messaging .

Enes Eryiğit :   focused on compatibility testing, 
ensuring the website functioned properly across different browsers and screen sizes.

Elnur Aliyev :   handled database and security testing. 
His role involved validating the integration with other 3rd party
platforms  , encryption, CRUD operations and  vulnerabilities.

Mohamad Attia :    functional testing,  
focusing on key workflows like registration, login, and form submissions, order system functionalities
ensuring everything behaved as expected from a user perspective.  ^b5yWkWIt

Scope:
   - Functional, usability, compatibility, database, and security testing

   - Out-of-scope: deep mobile support, full-scale automation, payment integration

Strategy:

    - Manual black-box testing + some tedting tools(jest,vitest ....)

    - automated testing

Schedule:

Testing phases ran from 12/2  to 3/5/2025, 
in stages like usability, functional, security, and performance. ^1YsIEshx

What were your testing objectives and what types of testing did you use (manual, automated, or both)? ^N7J8BxrD

What tools, frameworks, or resources did you use for testing? ^ABCowja0

Manual: Notion (to track the testcases), Chrome DevTools (+observation)

Automated: Selenium (UI), Vitest/Jest (frontend), Apache JMeter (load)

Security:  SQLMap

Compatibility: BrowserStack

Database: PostgreSQL via CLI ^8gJ0SHIA

How did you plan and organize your testing (scope, strategy, and schedule)? ^X36N9KHQ

What was your testing environment setup like (hardware/software)? ^0LyQ6EO7

✅ Passed: Registration, login, SQL injection protection, performance load tests

❌ Issues found:

    UI-01: No validation preventing form submission with missing fields (fiexed)

    SEC-01: Error messages revealed database schema details (fixed)

    IE11: Not working on Internet explorer 11 

    Performance under load was strong  (avg. 150 ms/query during 10-user simulation)
    (fixed) ^O4rv0Tep

What were the results of your tests — did you find any bugs or issues? ^MfEOaRjL

What did you learn from this testing process, and what would you improve next time? ^EHzMj54W

Through this project, we learned that while our stack (React + JavaScript + Node.js) was flexible, 
it introduced challenges as the project scales.

    Key takeaways:

        - Validation logic should be centralized  as  state and reused

        - Type safety helps prevent runtime errors

    Improvements for the future:

        - Using TypeScript to ensure the safety

        - Useing modern testing tools (Zod)
 
        - Implement CI/CD  for automated testing

        - It maybe better of using more Modern frameworks (Next,Nuxt)
          which have its own benefits like (ssr,ppr,image optimisation,cashing .....) ^SvhOdYET

🛠️ How We Plan to Fix the Bugs

    - Fix Missing Form Validation  :

      -- track the inputs so the users can’t submit empty forms.

      -- making the inpu as required fields and show its errors.

    - Hide Sensitive Error Messages  :

       -- Replace database errors with simple, safe messages.

       -- Keep  logs for developers only. .

    - Test Everything Again :

      -- After fixing bugs, retest using manual and automated tools again.

      -- Make sure fixes don’t break anything else. ^mZbD1sr5
